A flip card is the card a reader clicks to turn over. It was the **worst-covered widget in the library** — of 484 the writers tagged, **49 built, one in ten** — and the second-largest remaining class by modules, touching **191**. The reason was not that the writers' material was awkward: the converter knew **three** ways to read a flip-card table and the writers use at least **six**, so 220 declines died on one line asking "is this table exactly two columns wide?"; and a further 52 died because the builder **insisted every card carry a photograph**, which the human developers' own work flatly contradicts — across 643 gold groups, **43% of card fronts are text only**. **Flip cards now build 228 of 484 — 47.1%, up from 10.1%; overall interactive coverage 38.5% → 42.0%; NEW BUILDS 179 / LOST 0 in EVERY widget type; 105 modules.** The registry question that redirected round 281 was asked first again and answered immediately: **flipCard has no who-you-are gate at all.** The fix is `#flipCardCards`, a general composer tried LAST so all 49 pre-round builds are byte-identical by construction, resolving cards from an ordered delimiter vocabulary — face-marker rows that repeat, a face-marker label COLUMN, per-cell face markers riding with the content, a two-row column table (gold-verified on SSFUN03), N×2 rows, `[Flip Card N]` and `[front]`/`[back]` tags with **text-only fronts allowed**, face LABEL WORDS ("Facing:"/"Reverse:"/`[on flip]`), and a bare media series — over the round-278 accordion's member vocabulary, shared through default-inert options so the accordion and tabs cannot move. **THREE BUGS THE VERIFIER CAUGHT BEFORE THEY SHIPPED:** the writer's photo BRIEF rendering as visible card text, a bare URL rendering as a paragraph, and — in the verifier itself — the gold's `class="front flipImage"` faces, **1065 of them, never harvested at all**, which had been making correct builds read as defects. The verifier also gained the page-scoped A1 gold-substitution lane, **without weakening its protected criterion to let this round pass**: module-level divergence still fails, and the six modules whose gold carries no flip card anywhere are named in the changelog rather than reclassified. **The §9 protected gate holds-or-improves: divergence 0 in both states, builds 33 → 49, exact 13 → 21.** PROOF: the toggles-OFF census reproduces the r281 published numbers EXACTLY **and was re-run twice after the fixes changed the builder** (the first two figures would both have been wrong to publish); LOST 0 index-independent; byte identity one state per process with 5 out-of-class canaries identical in BOTH states; **the leak guard proven over ALL 105 affected modules in four batches, per-module identical every time**; tags 9557/9557 REAL 0; entry-parity PASS; index-sync 33/28; all seven widget selftests GREEN. **SAME ROUND, A STANDING TRAP FIXED:** the selftest fixture path collided with undeletable leftovers from earlier sessions, which is the `EACCES` failure the last several kickoffs told the next session to "just re-run"; it now carries a random suffix. All 256 remaining declines are named in the changelog — the biggest, 65, is loose prose with nothing marking where one card ends, and 41 are grids where **the gold invents the front**, which is never done. **RECOMMENDED, WAITING TO BE ASKED: `REGENERATE CORPUS - the 105 flipCard modules in outputs/_r282_affected.txt`** — by far the largest scoped regeneration the chain has produced; seven rounds are now pending. Env `FLIPCARDS_OFF`; data `interactive_builders.flipCard.general_cards`. PRIOR round below.)

- **Build:** `260618.53` (round 281 — **TABS: BUILD EVERY VARIATION** (Chris — the interactive-coverage chain, round 6 of 8: speechBubble ✅ → hint/hintSlider ✅ → accordion ✅ → carousel ✅ → modal ✅ → tabs ✅ → flipCard → clickDrop; **NO REGENERATION — not requested; the gates were NOT re-run and the round-275 baselines below still describe the corpus**). Tabs are the row of clickable headings a reader flicks between. Unlike every widget in this chain so far tabs already had **five** builders — and four of them were switched on only for a short list of subjects, mined from what the human developers happened to build. So the obvious suspicion was that tabs were being refused for WHO THE MODULE WAS rather than for anything wrong with the writer's material. **That suspicion was measured, and it was wrong: switching off every one of those gates moves tabs from 17 built to 18 — a gain of exactly ONE.** The gates were never the blocker, only the first door; the real blocker is that the writer usually marks the tabs out with a **table** (or a plain run of headings) no builder could read, and one member none of them could place threw away the whole widget. **Tabs now build 34 of 113 — 30.1%, up from 15.0%; overall interactive coverage 38.2% → 38.5%; NEW BUILDS 17 / LOST 0 in EVERY widget type; 15 modules.** The fix is `#tabsPanes`, a general composer tried LAST so all 17 pre-round builds are byte-identical by construction, resolving panes from an ordered delimiter vocabulary — `[Tab N]` tags · a table read COLUMN-major including a role-labelled first column (ENFUN01, whose gold ships exactly those tabs) · a table read ROW-major or one-pane-per-cell · a repeating heading run — over the round-278 accordion's member vocabulary, shared verbatim through a default-inert `delims` option so the accordion cannot move. **THREE GUARDS THE VERIFIER CAUGHT BEFORE THEY SHIPPED:** a flipCard `[front]`/`[drop]` table becoming four tabs all labelled "front" (and the fix had to be "wholly red", not "any red", or OSSC401 and ENFUN05 silently lose their builds — which is exactly what the blanket first cut did); duplicate labels; and an orphan `/` at the top of a pane. PROOF: the toggles-OFF census reproduces the r280 published numbers EXACTLY and was **re-run after those guards changed the builder**; LOST 0 proven index-independently; byte identity one state per process with 6 out-of-class canaries identical in BOTH states; **the leak guard proven over ALL 15 affected modules, per-module identical**; tags 9557/9557 REAL 0; entry-parity PASS; index-sync 33/28; every other widget selftest GREEN. The verifier itself gained three general rules — te-reo macron folding, label containment in either direction (the gold trims the HEAD as well as the tail), and the A1 gold-substitution lane made PAGE-scoped — leaving **7 named defects**, none a garble. All 79 remaining declines are named in the changelog; the largest, 19, is a bundle with no per-pane delimiter at all, and 16 are `[Tab N]` panes the gold labels with invented text ("Character 1…4") that is in no Writers Template — **a label is never invented**. **RECOMMENDED, waiting to be asked: `REGENERATE CORPUS - the 15 tabs modules in outputs/_r281_affected.txt`** — and like rounds 277–280 this one is skeleton-visible in one respect, since a peeled table title row emits a heading or paragraph outside the collapsed widget marker. Env `TABPANES_OFF`; data `interactive_builders.tabs.general_panes`. PRIOR round below.)

- **Build:** `260618.52` (round 280 — **MODAL: BUILD EVERY VARIATION** (Chris — the interactive-coverage chain, round 5 of 8: speechBubble ✅ → hint/hintSlider ✅ → accordion ✅ → carousel ✅ → modal ✅ → tabs → flipCard → clickDrop; **NO REGENERATION — not requested; the gates were NOT re-run and the round-275 baselines below still describe the corpus**). A modal is the pop-out a reader clicks something to open. The converter could build exactly two kinds: one where the clickable thing is an iStock photo, and one where the "modal" is really just a link to a PDF. It had **no builder at all for the ordinary kind — where the reader clicks a piece of text** — and that turns out to be the kind the human developers build most: **213 of the 283 pop-outs in the library, 75.3%, across 52 modules**. So a writer who typed a label and its pop-out text got a hand-off box, every time. **Modals now build 161 of 380 — 42.4%, up from 23.2%; overall interactive coverage 36.8% → 38.2%; NEW BUILDS 73 / LOST 0 in EVERY widget type; 34 modules.** The decline-reason recorder accounted for **100% of the 292 declines** and they collapsed onto just two guards, both belonging to the round-73 button path. Two fixes: `#modalSets`, tried LAST so all 88 pre-round builds are byte-identical by construction, emitting the corpus convention (`div.button.TKmodalButton` + its sibling `div.TKmodal`) from four delimiters — numbered `[Modal N …]` sub-tags, the same-block label line, a table row, and the tag's own text (`MODALSETS_OFF`); and the scanner recovering the first set's label from the line above (`MODALLEAD_OFF`). The round-73 button path was extracted unchanged so its refusals FALL THROUGH instead of ending the dispatch — the round-279 dead-end lesson. **THREE BUGS THE PROBE CAUGHT BEFORE THEY SHIPPED:** a crash on every image set (the wrong template block was passed, and `Build`'s catch swallowed it); **a multiple-choice quiz being turned into a row of buttons** (XGF9004 — only the correct option carries the pop-out and the distractors are plain lines between the tags, so D2 now requires a strict label/modal alternation and XGF9004 correctly keeps its box, its gold shipping no modal at all); and the scanner absorb firing where the builder then declined, trapping text in a box that never built — fixed by making the scanner read the BUILDER's own gold-backed label cap. PROOF: the toggles-OFF census reproduces the r279 published numbers EXACTLY; LOST 0 proven index-independently; byte identity one state per process, with the 5 out-of-class canaries identical in BOTH states and all 12 changed pages inside the affected set; **the leak guard proven over ALL 34 affected modules in three batches, per-module identical every time**; tags 9557/9557 REAL 0; entry-parity PASS; index-sync 33/28; flipCard 33/div 0; all widget selftests GREEN. All 219 remaining declines named in the changelog — the biggest, 80, is a modal the writer never gave a trigger for, and a label is never invented. **RECOMMENDED, waiting to be asked: `REGENERATE CORPUS - the 34 modal modules in outputs/_r280_affected.txt`** — and like rounds 277–279 this one is skeleton-visible in one respect, since the `div.button.TKmodalButton` trigger sits outside the collapsed widget marker. **The modal is also the first widget in this chain with NO `_verify_*.cjs` — writing one is a recorded follow-up.** Env `MODALSETS_OFF`/`MODALLEAD_OFF`; data `interactive_builders.modal.modal_sets`, `member_rule.same_block_label_absorb`. PRIOR round below.)

- **Build:** `260618.51` (round 279 — **CAROUSEL: BUILD EVERY VARIATION** (Chris — the interactive-coverage chain, round 4 of 8: speechBubble ✅ → hint/hintSlider ✅ → accordion ✅ → carousel ✅ → modal → tabs → flipCard → clickDrop; **NO REGENERATION — not requested; the gates were NOT re-run and the round-275 baselines below still describe the corpus**). A carousel is the slideshow a reader clicks through, and writers lay one out in two ways: as a list of tagged items, or — very often — as a plain **table**, one slide per row. The converter could read the table only if it matched one of three narrow shapes, and worse, the branch that tried first **returned its refusal as the final answer**, so a table that missed by an inch was never offered to any other builder at all. That single dead-end accounted for **143 of the 561 declines across 75 modules**. **Carousels now build 639 of 896 — 71.3%, up from 35.4%; overall interactive coverage 30.5% → 36.8%; NEW BUILDS 331 / LOST 0 in EVERY widget type; 144 modules.** Four fixes: the table-slide fallback that reads any table by the gold's own rule — one data ROW is one slide (`CARTABLE_OFF`, tried LAST so the 308 pre-round builds are byte-identical by construction); a captured table with another carousel ahead now ENDS the walk, so XTAS101's three slideshows are three carousels exactly as the gold has them (`CARSPLIT_OFF`); the member vocabulary that names a non-iStock image, an unembeddable video, a `[Caption]` and an `[audio]` (`CARMEMBER_OFF`); and "carousel + carousel" recognised as only the placeholder label (`CARSAMETYPE_OFF`). **FOUR BUGS THE PROBE CAUGHT BEFORE THEY SHIPPED:** a leading-row test borrowed from round 63 that ate every one of AGH1002's data rows; the shared instruction vocabulary being too broad to strip prose with (it contains "below", "above", "note"); a newly-reachable build putting `[Embed audio book]` on the page; and a reo module's bilingual phonics grid becoming a slideshow. PROOF: the toggles-OFF census reproduces the r278 published numbers EXACTLY; LOST 0 proven index-independently (the scanner change renumbers bundles); byte identity one state per process with 6 out-of-class canaries identical in both and no pagination churn; **the leak guard proven over ALL 144 affected modules in four batches, per-module identical every time**; `_verify_carousel.cjs` 107 built / 172 video slides / 12 mismatched of which 10 are A/B-proven pre-existing, selftest GREEN; tags 9557/9557 REAL 0; entry-parity PASS; index-sync 33/28. All 257 remaining declines named in the changelog. **RECOMMENDED, waiting to be asked: `REGENERATE CORPUS - the 144 carousel modules in outputs/_r279_affected.txt`** — this round is skeleton-visible in one respect, since prose and buttons released around a split carousel sit outside any widget marker. Env `CARTABLE_OFF`/`CARSPLIT_OFF`/`CARMEMBER_OFF`/`CARSAMETYPE_OFF`; data `carousel.table_slides`, `carousel.same_type_merge`, `rich_slides.member_vocabulary`, `member_rule.carousel_table_terminates_before_next`. PRIOR round below.)

- **Build:** `260618.50` (round 278 — **ACCORDION: BUILD EVERY VARIATION** (Chris — the interactive-coverage chain, round 3 of 8: speechBubble ✅ → hint/hintSlider ✅ → accordion ✅ → carousel → modal → tabs → flipCard → clickDrop; **NO REGENERATION — not requested; the gates were NOT re-run and the round-275 baselines below still describe the corpus**). An accordion is a stack of click-to-open panels, and the converter could only find those panels ONE way: the writer had to type `[accordion 1]`, `[accordion 2]` above each. Writers do that about half the time. The rest put the panels in a **table**, used a **repeating heading**, or typed each panel as a line beginning with a **bold label** — and every one of those shipped as a hand-off box, though the human builds an accordion from all four. The decline recorder (the r276 tool re-pointed, so the shipped builder names its own verdict) accounted for **100% of the 454 declines**, and its top four guards turned out to be ONE mechanism: **239 declines / 105 modules where content arrived before any panel opened.** **Accordions now build 371 of 707 — 52.5%, up from 35.8%; overall interactive coverage 28.1% → 30.5%; NEW BUILDS 126 / LOST 0 in EVERY widget type; 69 modules / 91 pages.** Two fixes: the panel-delimiter vocabulary in a fallback that runs LAST so the 253 pre-round builds are byte-identical by construction (`ACCPANELS_OFF`), and a trailing `[button]` ending the capture so it renders as a REAL button instead of being swallowed (`ACCBTNTAIL_OFF` — zero-risk by construction: not one building accordion carries a button, and the gold puts one inside a panel in only 1.7% of 3,099 panels). **TWO BUGS THE PROBE CAUGHT BEFORE THEY SHIPPED:** the bare `[accordion]` opener matching the panel pattern (which collapsed CEDO102/ENGI303 into one heading-less panel), and a whole sentence shipped inside `<h4>` (CEDW101). **AND A THIRD CAUGHT BY RUNNING IT:** releasing a go-to-journal button re-created the duplicate button Chris reported at round 273, because r239 already owns that case — the fence costs XGF9003 two builds and is accepted. PROOF: the toggles-OFF census reproduces the r277 published numbers EXACTLY; LOST 0 proven index-independently (the scanner change shifts bundle indices); byte identity one state per process with 6 out-of-class canaries identical in both; **the leak guard proven by running the defect audit's own predicate in memory — 1,313 leaks in BOTH states, per-module identical**; `_verify_accordion.cjs` over all 69 affected modules 523 panels / defect 0 / every panel matches the human; tags 9557/9557 REAL 0; entry-parity PASS; index-sync 33/28. Env `ACCPANELS_OFF`/`ACCBTNTAIL_OFF`; data `interactive_builders.accordion.panel_delimiters`, `member_rule.button_tail_terminates`. PRIOR round below.)

Chris asked for a GENERAL fix (any tag, missing open OR close). **Root cause = an asymmetry:** `TagNormaliser.Parse` (the classifier) already repairs both edge forms — dangling `[H2` and leading `H2]` — proven `Parse(" H2] ")`→h2; but ~10 OTHER red-span consumers (`RenderText` whose regex needs a real `[`, the overview-menu partition, the black-tag strip, `InteractiveScanner`…) read the raw brackets and leak the literal. **Fix:** `DocxExtractor.RepairContentTags(blocks, normaliser)` — wired into `TrimFrontMatter`, the ONE choke point both `App.js` + `batch_convert` call — inserts the missing `[` ONCE into `block.text` when a CONTENT red span opens with a lone `x]` whose token is WHOLLY a known tag (Parse `how` ∈ `clean_hows` = exact/denumbered/denumbered_head; `how="embedded"` rejects a tag word buried in prose). Original case preserved; every downstream consumer then sees a clean `[x]`. GENERAL across all tags (lexicon-driven). **MISSING-CLOSE (`[hover: def`) left untouched** — `Parse.dangling` + `RenderText`'s optional `]?` already absorb it (0 clean render-leaks) and it is the deferred split-bracket infoTrigger lever (§15). **Measured 197-set** (`outputs/_measure_malformed_brackets.cjs` + `_agg_malformed.cjs`): 721 malformed red spans / 116 modules — missing-close 559 (untouched), missing-open 162 (159 at start) of which **~73 clean / 37 modules** are repaired and **47 embedded prose false-positives** correctly declined (`that when they click/hover…]`→audio, `Please create a dropdown menu…]`→dropdown). ## 15. Remaining levers (sized; not yet built)

- **SPLIT-bracket inline infoTrigger — FREE-BODY class DONE (round 201); the cv2-DUMP residue is the widget-building backlog.**
  Round 201 MEASURED the class honestly (`outputs/_measure_infosplit.py`, `_probe_infosplit_leak.cjs`): the
  hover/rollover/definition family has **428 raw literal `[…]` leaks / 111 modules** (this is the "761/108"
  the round-75 count was pointing at) but **389 (91%) are TRAPPED inside un-built-widget cv2 placeholder dumps**
  — the human built the widget as live content with hovers; the converter left the whole widget as a
  placeholder, so its raw markers dump into the box. A free-body weave CANNOT reach those. The honest VISIBLE
  FREE-BODY footprint was **39 leaks / 19 modules**, and round 201 SHIPPED the re-stitch for it
  (`ListsAndRuns.hoverStitch`, env `INFOSPLIT_OFF`): 12 modules woven, visible footprint 39→16 (the 16 are
  bare-`[definition]` / instruction / URL / heading-anchor / long-quote DECLINES). REMAINING lever = the
  **389 cv2-dump-trapped markers**: recovering those means BUILDING the widget (accordion/tabs/clickDrop with
  its captured hover members), not a free-body weave — the widget-building backlog (ENGJ302's fact-file modal,
  CEDT501's clickDrop, MXDI301, …). Also-recorded free-body residue: ARFUN03's cross-STRUCTURE heading anchor
  (`[Hover on locomotor: …]` whose term is in a separate `[H2]`), HIS1004's standalone long-quote def. Tools:
  `outputs/_measure_infosplit.py`, `_measure_infosplit_verdict.py`, `_probe_infosplit_leak.cjs`,
  `_measure_infotrigger.py`, `_probe_trigger.cjs`.
- **Face-member raw-table CAPTURE — XGF `[back]` (round 59) + PNR `[Item N]` `[Embedded]` pattern (round 60) DONE.**

  captured (`#interactiveInTable` + `face_table_capture`; `FACETABLE_OFF`). PNR101/102 `[Embedded]→[Item N]`

  / clickDrop 4 / carousel 5 / tabs 2 / slider 2 / …; `reference/tests/body_compare.json`). No single

  shift (66% already all-match, 18% MIXED with 0/+1/+2/+3 offsets within a page) — the human

## 16. Working discipline / gotchas

- **Never expose internal `/sessions/<id>/…` paths** to the user — say "the connected folder".
- **Handover/kickoff files are SELF-DELETING (Chris, 2026-07-10).** If a session writes a
  `NEW_SESSION_MESSAGE__*.md` (or any one-off handover file) for a future session, that file's
  lifecycle ends when its round ships: the RECEIVING session deletes it in the finalise ritual
  (§12 step 5). Write kickoffs only when a handover genuinely needs one; never let them pile up
  at the project root — the changelog + STOCKTAKE ledger are the durable records, not kickoffs.
  **When GENERATING a kickoff file, the file itself MUST open with an explicit self-delete
  notice** — a top-of-file banner telling the receiving session, in so many words, that it must
  delete the file once the message has run and its round(s) are resolved (shipped, declined with
  the measurement recorded, or superseded — the changelog entry is the durable record either way).
  A multi-ticket kickoff is deleted by the session that resolves its LAST ticket. Never write a
  kickoff without this banner (template: the header of
  `NEW_SESSION_MESSAGE__Menu_Tabs_Follow_Ups.md`, or any prior kickoff's ⚠️ block).
- **The engine has ONE copy on disk, inside the git repo** (`pageforge-site/converter-v2/`); the dev
  harness `CONVERTER_V2/` reaches it via symlinks (see §4). Edit the engine through either path — they
  are the same file. Because the symlinks keep every path stable, a comments/consolidation-only change
  MUST regenerate the corpus byte-for-byte (proven round 204 by an empty `_content_manifest.py diff`).
- **`publish-converter-to-site.sh` is RETIRED** (`_Backups/pre_consolidation_2026-07-09/`). It did
  `rm -rf $DEST/app`, which would now delete the real engine. Do NOT resurrect it. Publishing is a git
  commit from `pageforge-site/` — **never `git commit`/`git push` without Chris's explicit go-ahead.**
- **The connected folder is a FUSE mount:** deletes are blocked until `allow_cowork_file_delete` is
  granted. `batch_convert.cjs` `rm`s each output dir before rewriting, so **a regen silently fails with
  `EPERM: unlink` until delete permission is granted** (the wrapper then reports every module "stale").
  Grant it up front for any regen. A bare `git status` also needs it, or it leaves a `.git/index.lock`.
- The corpus is **shared**: a Cowork project pointed at `FINAL_MODULE_DATA` edits the same files,
  so the on-disk state can advance between sessions — **trust `BUILD_CHANGELOG.md` + the live
  files over any remembered numbers** (this file's snapshots can lag a round).
- **Scoped ship (§10a) is safe ONLY if the fix has a faithful `*_OFF` toggle and the detector is
  COMPLETE.** The toggle-OFF invariant is the whole safety argument — a fix shipped without one MUST
  use the full ship. The affected-set detector must scan **all 390 WTs**, never `compare_set` (197):
  the round-174 probe scoped to `compare_set` under-scoped by 15 modules incl. the headline ENGC101.
  When unsure a change is corpus-wide, use the full `ship.sh` — the 60% guard and the cadence ledger
  are hints, not a substitute for judgement.
- **NEVER `git checkout`/`git restore` a file in `pageforge-site` without first proving the work in it is
  COMMITTED (round 284).** `git log -1 --format=%ci` + `git status` take five seconds. Rounds 276–283 lived
  as uncommitted working-tree changes for eight rounds; a `git checkout` intended to undo an accidental
  whitespace reformat silently reverted `Emit_Templates.json` to round 275 and destroyed all ten of the
  chain's data blocks — not in the index, the stash, the reflog, any dangling blob, or either remote.
  Recovery cost most of a session and still left a 28-page residual. **Ship discipline: commit
  `pageforge-site` at the end of every shipped round** (Chris pushes).
- **Never `json.dumps` round-trip a data file (round 284).** `data/*.json` is TAB-indented; a
  `json.load` -> `json.dumps(indent=1)` rewrite converts every tab to spaces and produces a ~4,900-line
  phantom diff on `Emit_Templates.json` — which is what provoked the `git checkout` above. Edit data
  files with the Edit tool or a surgical text insertion that preserves the indentation
  (`outputs/_r284_restore_blocks.py` is the pattern), and diff before trusting the result.
- **To learn what a data block owes the engine, PROXY it, don't grep it (round 284).**
  `outputs/_r284_missing_keys.cjs` deep-proxies `interactive_builders` and records every key the engine
  READS and finds `undefined`, with the path. Regex-scanning for `cfg.x ?? default` misses reads reached
  through helpers and aliased locals — it missed `modal_sets.image_mode_P`, a bare template read whose
  absence silently declined every image-triggered modal set.
- **NEVER `sed -i` (or any in-place rewrite) a SYMLINKED file (round 276).** `CONVERTER_V2/CLAUDE.md`
  and `CONVERTER_V2/BUILD_CHANGELOG.md` are symlinks into `pageforge-site/converter-v2/`; `sed -i`
  writes a temp file and RENAMES over the path, silently replacing the symlink with a regular file
  and forking the two copies. Edit the REAL path (`pageforge-site/converter-v2/…`) — the Edit tool
  refuses a symlink for exactly this reason. If it has already happened: fix the real file, then
  `rm` the fork and `ln -s ../pageforge-site/converter-v2/<name>` (the `rm` needs
  `allow_cowork_file_delete`), and `diff` the two paths to confirm.
- **`_stalecheck.sh`'s data scan is SCOPED to the engine's `data_map` (round 288).** It used to
  treat every `data/*.json` as output-affecting, so rebuilding the DIAGNOSTIC
  `Module_Feature_Index.json` — which §13 requires after every regeneration — declared all 413
  modules stale seconds after a clean 0-stale run, on every full ship (recorded at r275, fixed
  at r288). It now reads `app/js/_modules.json`'s `data_map` (19 files) and falls back to the
  whole directory if that read fails. Proven both ways: 0 stale after a feature-index rebuild,
  413 stale after touching `Emit_Templates.json` or an engine `.js`. **The same over-broad scan
  is still in `_coverage_dashboard.py`'s freshness guard — narrow it the same way.**
- **`_stalecheck.sh`'s trailing slashes are LOAD-BEARING (round 228).** `CONVERTER_V2/app`
  and `CONVERTER_V2/data` are symlinks; GNU find (default -P) does NOT traverse a symlink
  given as the FINAL path component, so `find ../../data` silently saw nothing from
  2026-07-09 → round 228 (`../../app/js` only worked because its symlink is interior to
  the argument). Any new tool that `find`s through the symlinked dirs MUST end the path
  argument with `/` (or use `-H`). Same trap: `find CONVERTER_V2/data -…` finds nothing;
  `find CONVERTER_V2/data/ -…` works.
- **0-stale means CONTENT, not mtime, under a scoped regen.** Untouched modules are older-by-timestamp
  yet byte-identical (round 148's false alarm) — judge with `_content_manifest.py fresh`, not
  `_stalecheck.sh`, after a scoped regen. `outputs/_content_manifest.txt` is the byte-identity ground
  truth; keep it refreshed by every ship (`ship.sh` / `scoped_ship.sh --commit`).
- Prefer `Read`/`Edit`/`Write` for files; use the sandbox `bash` for regen, gates, grep, unzip.
- Don't read an output-format skill (docx/pptx/xlsx/pdf) for this work — the deliverable is the
  converter's HTML, measured by the gates, not a document export.
- When in doubt about whether a difference is a bug or editorial: measure the class; if there's no
  derivable discriminator, it's (C) editorial — leave it and record the decline (the loop has
  prevented more bad changes than it has shipped).
```
